.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_is_stable:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **clock_is_stable**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_is_stable>`

Whether :ref:`CLOCK_MONITOR_RTT_JITTER<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_MONITOR_RTT_JITTER>` is below :ref:`CLOCK_PARAM_JITTER_STABILITY_THRESHOLD<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_PARAM_JITTER_STABILITY_THRESHOLD>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_is_synchronized:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **clock_is_synchronized**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_is_synchronized>`

Whether a calibration has landed. The first :ref:`clock_ingest_pong()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_ingest_pong>` sets it and hard-aligns, so :ref:`SYNC_MODE_STRETCH<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_SYNC_MODE_STRETCH>` begins already converged.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_physics_step:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_physics_step**\ (\ delta\: :godot:`float`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_physics_step>`

Pays ``delta`` seconds of frame time into the tick schedule and announces whatever whole ticks it buys.

The remainder is banked rather than dropped, so two half frames are worth exactly one tick and no time is invented or lost. A frame longer than :ref:`CLOCK_PARAM_STALL_THRESHOLD<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_PARAM_STALL_THRESHOLD>` is read as a hitch rather than as simulated time and resets the accumulator, and no frame may buy more than :ref:`CLOCK_PARAM_MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_PARAM_MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME>`.

A session that consumed a clock configuration calls this itself, once per :godot:`SceneTree` physics frame, with that frame's actual delta. A clock left at :ref:`CLOCK_PARAM_MANUAL_TICK<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_PARAM_MANUAL_TICK>` ignores that pump so a rig can own the schedule.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_request_handshake:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_request_handshake**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_request_handshake>`

Asks server authority for its tickrate over the clock's handshake channel, so the two rates can be compared before any tick is calibrated against them. The server returns its own tickrate, and a reply that disagrees is what :ref:`clock_set_mismatch_action()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_mismatch_action>` decides the response to.

Only a peer that is not server authority calls this. The session runs it once per transport generation, as soon as a connection to the server exists, so a host never asks itself.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_send_ping:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_send_ping**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_send_ping>`

Sends the local wall-clock timestamp to server authority over the clock's ping channel, unreliably, because a lost sample is cheaper than a late one. The server echoes it back with its own tick, and that reply lands as :ref:`clock_pong_received<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_clock_pong_received>` once :ref:`clock_ingest_pong()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_ingest_pong>` has processed it.

The handshake's reply triggers the first ping, and later pings repeat on whatever schedule drives recalibration.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_gate:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_set_gate**\ (\ armed\: :godot:`bool`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_gate>`

Arms a simulation gate when ``armed`` is ``true`` and releases one otherwise. Gates nest, so the world resumes on the release that matches the first arm.

The physics server runs exactly one step per frame and takes no argument about how long that step is, so the only way a clock can make a tick worth the same amount of time on two peers is to decide which frames the world may step at all. A gated clock pays each announced tick into a budget of whole steps and spends one per simulated frame, and a frame with nothing to spend holds.

.. code:: text

    clock_set_gate(true)                a body the physics server integrates
      tick announced -> credit += clock_get_physics_steps_per_tick()
      frame spends   -> credit -= 1, clock_is_simulating() true
      no credit      -> clock_is_simulating() false, the world holds
    clock_set_gate(false)               the last release restores every frame

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_mismatch_action:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_set_mismatch_action**\ (\ action\: :ref:`MismatchAction<enum_NetwMultiplayer_MismatchAction>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_mismatch_action>`

What a session does when a clock handshake reply carries a tickrate different from its own, one of the :ref:`MismatchAction<enum_NetwMultiplayer_MismatchAction>` values.

\ :ref:`MISMATCH_ACTION_WARN<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_MISMATCH_ACTION_WARN>` is the default because a tickrate difference is a configuration mistake rather than a protocol violation, and a session that disconnects on it gives a game no way to say so.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **clock_set_param**\ (\ param\: :ref:`ClockParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_ClockParam>`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_param>`

Turns one tunable clock knob, returning :godot:`@GlobalScope.OK <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_OK>`.

Rejects :ref:`CLOCK_PARAM_TICKRATE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_CLOCK_PARAM_TICKRATE>` with :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNAUTHORIZED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNAUTHORIZED>`, because a tickrate that differs between two peers means their ticks are worth different amounts of simulated time. It is settable through the :ref:`NetwClockConfig<class_NetwClockConfig>` a game declares with :ref:`Netw.configure_clock()<class_Netw_method_configure_clock>` alone, and a peer that connects carrying a different one is reported by :ref:`clock_tickrate_mismatch<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_clock_tickrate_mismatch>`.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                     the knob was turned
    ┠╴ERR_UNAUTHORIZED       param was CLOCK_PARAM_TICKRATE
    ┖╴ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER  param names no known knob

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_synchronized:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_set_synchronized**\ (\ value\: :godot:`bool`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_set_synchronized>`

Declares whether the calibration still holds. A client that loses its server clears this so the next handshake calibrates from scratch rather than nudging a stale target.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_step:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_step**\ (\ count\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_step>`

Advances the clock by ``count`` whole ticks, announcing each one exactly as a pumped frame would.

This and :ref:`clock_physics_step()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_physics_step>` announce identically, so a stepped rig and a running session cannot mean different things by a tick. They differ in one respect. A step is not a frame, so this brackets no :ref:`clock_before_tick_loop<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_clock_before_tick_loop>`, and a caller that needs the bracket opens it with :ref:`clock_tick_loop()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_tick_loop>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_tick_loop:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **clock_tick_loop**\ (\ open\: :godot:`bool`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_clock_tick_loop>`

Opens the frame bracket when ``open`` is ``true`` and closes it otherwise, announcing :ref:`clock_before_tick_loop<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_clock_before_tick_loop>` and :ref:`clock_after_tick_loop<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_clock_after_tick_loop>`.

A frame-tier entity authors, sends and consumes in that pass, so a rig that steps ticks without bracketing them leaves such an entity at drive zero however many ticks it runs.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_complete_auth:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **complete_auth**\ (\ id\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_complete_auth>`

Ends ``id``'s authentication handshake on :ref:`inner<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_inner>`, moving it out of :ref:`get_authenticating_peers()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_get_authenticating_peers>` and into the connected roster. Returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNCONFIGURED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNCONFIGURED>` when this session wraps no :ref:`inner<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_inner>`, and otherwise whatever the wrapped :godot:`SceneMultiplayer` returns.

\ :ref:`send_auth()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_send_auth>` carries the handshake's own data, and this is the call that closes it. Both keep :godot:`SceneMultiplayer`'s own spelling, so a game swapping one session for the other finds the authentication handshake under the same names.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_core_of:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`NetwMultiplayer<class_NetwMultiplayer>` **core_of**\ (\ node\: :godot:`Node`\ ) |static| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_core_of>`

The same session :ref:`of()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_of>` resolves, as the **NetwMultiplayer** itself rather than as the object it publishes. This is what a caller wants when it is about to call a session verb, and :ref:`of()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_of>` is what it wants when it is about to compare against an installed :godot:`MultiplayerAPI`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_disconnect_peer:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **disconnect_peer**\ (\ id\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_disconnect_peer>`

Closes ``id``'s connection outright, with no authority check and no notice sent first. Keeps :godot:`SceneMultiplayer`'s own spelling, so a game swapping one session for the other closes a peer the same way.

\ :ref:`peer_kick()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_peer_kick>` is the server-authority call built on this. It rejects without :ref:`is_host<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_is_host>`, announces :ref:`peer_kicked<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_peer_kicked>` to the peer, and only then closes it. This is the bare call underneath, for a caller that has already made those decisions itself.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_declare:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **display_declare**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, comp\: :godot:`int`, track\: :godot:`StringName`, spec\: :ref:`NetwInterpolate<class_NetwInterpolate>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_declare>`

Declares ``track`` on ``entity``'s component ``comp``, wiring ``spec`` as the interpolation the display pump applies to that property and marking the entity's display state dirty so the wiring takes effect. Returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST>` when ``entity`` is not registered, :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_INVALID_DATA <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_INVALID_DATA>` when ``track`` is empty or ``spec`` is ``null``, and :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNAVAILABLE <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNAVAILABLE>` when ``comp`` names no live node on ``entity``.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the track was declared
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  entity is not registered
    ┠╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    track is empty, or spec is null
    ┖╴ERR_UNAVAILABLE     comp names no live node on entity

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Variant` **display_get_param**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, param\: :ref:`DisplayParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_DisplayParam>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_param>`

Reads back the display setting named by ``param`` for ``entity``, the same :ref:`DisplayParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_DisplayParam>` vocabulary :ref:`display_set_param()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_param>` writes, from :ref:`DISPLAY_PARAM_ROLE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_DISPLAY_PARAM_ROLE>` through :ref:`DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT>`. Returns ``null`` when ``entity`` has no committed display declaration.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_track_stat:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Variant` **display_get_track_stat**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, track\: :godot:`StringName`, stat\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_track_stat>`

Reads one live display measurement for ``entity``. A stat about the whole display ignores ``track``, and a stat about one channel uses it to select a declared property.

.. code:: text

    the whole display   pump_mode  pumped_frames  channels
                        ambiguous  role  starvation_ticks
                        display_lag
    one track           sleeping  offset_armed  offset_held
                        buffer  buffer_size

\ An unknown stat or an entity with no runtime returns ``null``.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_value:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Variant` **display_get_value**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, track\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_get_value>`

The last value written to ``entity``'s display channel ``track``, as :ref:`display_write_default()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_write_default>` or :ref:`display_snap()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_snap>` left it. Returns ``null`` when ``entity`` has no live display runtime or ``track`` names no declared channel.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_reset:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **display_reset**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_reset>`

Clears what ``entity`` displays, applies the live source values to its visual, and re-seats the playhead at the session's current display offset.

A predicted display that teleports the body snaps its visual to the new truth through this, so the smoothing resumes from where the body now is rather than gliding across the correction.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_callback:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **display_set_callback**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, callback\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_callback>`

Replaces the :godot:`Callable` :ref:`display_write_default()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_write_default>` calls in place of writing ``entity``'s target node or item directly, a no-op when ``entity`` is not registered. A valid ``callback`` is called with ``(entity, track, value)`` on every default write, and an invalid one clears it. Installing a callback drops any :ref:`display_set_target_item()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_target_item>` item, because the two routes are exclusive.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **display_set_param**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, param\: :ref:`DisplayParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_DisplayParam>`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_param>`

Writes ``value`` to the display setting named by ``param`` for ``entity``, covering every :ref:`DisplayParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_DisplayParam>` constant from :ref:`DISPLAY_PARAM_ROLE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_DISPLAY_PARAM_ROLE>` through :ref:`DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT>`. :ref:`DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_DISPLAY_PARAM_VISUAL_ROOT>` reads its value two ways. A :godot:`NodePath` or :godot:`String` is stored as written, which is what lets a visual be named before the node exists, and any other value is read as a component id and resolved through :ref:`display_set_target_item()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_target_item>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_target_item:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **display_set_target_item**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, item\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_target_item>`

Names ``item``, a :godot:`RenderingServer` canvas item or instance, as what :ref:`display_write_default()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_write_default>` writes ``entity``'s pose to when no node target and no :ref:`display_set_callback()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_set_callback>` callback take precedence. An invalid ``item`` clears the target. Setting an item drops any installed callback, because the two routes are exclusive.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_snap:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **display_snap**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, track\: :godot:`StringName`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_snap>`

Forces ``entity``'s display channel ``track`` to ``value`` immediately, bypassing the interpolation the channel would otherwise apply. A no-op when ``entity`` has no live display runtime or ``track`` names no declared channel.

A display that teleports rather than glides through a correction calls this so the visible pose jumps with the body instead of smoothing across the correction.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_write_default:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **display_write_default**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, track\: :godot:`StringName`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_display_write_default>`

The stock body of :ref:`_display_write()<class_NetwMultiplayer_private_method__display_write>`. It resolves the declared display ``track`` for ``entity`` and writes ``value`` through that track's target.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the write landed, or a callback consumed it
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  the track resolves to no target
    ┖╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    value is neither a Transform2D nor a Transform3D

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_adopt_inner:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **embed_adopt_inner**\ (\ inner\: :godot:`SceneMultiplayer`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_adopt_inner>`

Replaces :ref:`inner<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_inner>` in place, rebinding every transport edge this session listens on and re-emits, and re-applying the session's auth configuration to the new transport.

An embedding calls this when the :godot:`SceneMultiplayer` under a live session has to change without the session itself being torn down and rebuilt. An auth callback already set on ``inner`` is adopted, and one carrying none leaves this session's callback alone.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_dispose:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **embed_dispose**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_dispose>`

Releases the whole graph this session owns, and is the one teardown call an embedding makes.

It runs once however many callers ask, returning immediately when the teardown has already begun. It first drains everything already scheduled, the way :ref:`session_flush_deferred()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_session_flush_deferred>` does, because a session that ends has no next frame and the nodes that work was written against are still standing here. Only then does it release the scenes, the session state, the display runtimes, the replication of every property, the registered calls, the services and the roster.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_is_disposing:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **embed_is_disposing**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_is_disposing>`

Whether this session has started a deliberate shutdown. This distinguishes local shutdown from a lost server connection.

False for the whole life of a session that is still in use, and true from the first moment of teardown onward. A session never comes back from it.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_offer_bare_level:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **embed_offer_bare_level**\ (\ level\: :godot:`Node`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_offer_bare_level>`

Records ``level`` as the default single scene a scoped embedding offers, consumed by the next :ref:`embed_settle()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_settle>`.

The embedding captures it synchronously, so a node dropped in afterwards never becomes the candidate. A root install offers nothing and passes ``null``.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_phase:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`EmbedPhase<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EmbedPhase>` **embed_phase**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_phase>`

The current bootstrap phase of this session's authoring, advanced once by the installing embedding through :ref:`embed_settle()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_settle>`. Pairs with :ref:`embed_phase_changed<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_embed_phase_changed>`.

This is a different question from :ref:`state<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_state>`. A session at :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_LIVE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_LIVE>` is still :ref:`SESSION_STATE_OFFLINE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_SESSION_STATE_OFFLINE>` until it hosts or joins, because this returns how far the embedding has come and :ref:`state<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_state>` returns whether the session is connected.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_poll_transport:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **embed_poll_transport**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_poll_transport>`

Sends and receives one batch of datagrams, then sweeps what arriving traffic retires. Returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNCONFIGURED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNCONFIGURED>` when the session holds no :ref:`inner<class_NetwMultiplayer_property_inner>`.

A datagram is only sent or received here, so whatever drives this call decides how often a peer's simulation can see the network. Engine polling alone drives it once per rendered frame, which makes a peer's framerate govern its neighbour's input arrival. Commands pile up and land in clumps, and a clumped arrival reaches a body as a transition that spent the wrong amount of physics rather than as latency.

So the transport is also serviced once per tick of the session's clock, which is the guarantee this needs. Servicing a peer more often than once per simulated tick is harmless and only lowers latency, so the engine poll keeps its call and a session with no clock is unaffected.

.. code:: text

    clock_before_tick_loop   record the frame that closed
      clock_on_tick          tick_step
      clock_after_tick       flush this tick's frames, then embed_poll_transport()
    clock_after_tick_loop    drive and consume, against a queue the tick just filled

\ Safe to call again from game code that wants the newest inbound state before it runs.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_settle:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **embed_settle**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_settle>`

Resolves this session's authored declarations as one ordered step and advances :ref:`embed_phase()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_embed_phase>` to :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_LIVE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_LIVE>`.

Every session runs the same :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_DECLARING<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_DECLARING>` to :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_LIVE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_LIVE>` sequence, so the order declarations resolve in never depends on the order :godot:`Node._enter_tree() <Node#class_Node_private_method__enter_tree>` happens to visit them. It is called once at the first idle frame after the session is installed. A :ref:`MultiplayerTree<class_MultiplayerTree>` defers it from :godot:`Node._enter_tree() <Node#class_Node_private_method__enter_tree>` so the call lands after every declaration around it, and the autoload calls it on a one-shot :godot:`SceneTree.process_frame <SceneTree#class_SceneTree_signal_process_frame>`.

A call past :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_DECLARING<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_DECLARING>` returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.OK <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_OK>` and resolves nothing again. It returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNCONFIGURED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNCONFIGURED>` when a channel finished with no handler registered for it, and the phase still reaches :ref:`EMBED_PHASE_LIVE<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EMBED_PHASE_LIVE>`, because an unhandled channel is worth reporting and is not a reason to hold every declaration open.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                the phase reached LIVE, every channel had a handler
    ┖╴ERR_UNCONFIGURED  the phase still reached LIVE, but a peer-scoped
                       channel finished with no handler registered

::

    api.embed_offer_bare_level(level)   # optional default single scene
    api.embed_settle()                  # DECLARING -> SETTLING -> LIVE

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_add:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **endpoint_add**\ (\ transport\: :godot:`RID`, address\: :godot:`String`, display_name\: :godot:`String` = ""\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_add>`

Adds a row for ``address`` on ``transport`` and returns its handle, announcing :ref:`endpoint_added<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_endpoint_added>` when the row is new, invalid when ``transport`` names nothing this session holds.

::

    var endpoint := api.endpoint_add(
            api.transport_find(&"ENetMultiplayerPeer"),
            "203.0.113.42:21253")
    api.session_prepare_join(&"Dev", [])
    api.multiplayer_peer = peer

\ A handle identifies one address and transport pair. Adding the same pair returns the existing handle without emitting a signal.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_find:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **endpoint_find**\ (\ transport\: :godot:`RID`, address\: :godot:`String`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_find>`

The handle for the row ``transport`` and ``address`` name, invalid when this session holds no such row.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_get_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Variant` **endpoint_get_param**\ (\ endpoint\: :godot:`RID`, param\: :ref:`EndpointParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EndpointParam>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_get_param>`

One field of the row ``endpoint`` names, named by ``param``. ``null`` when this session holds no such row.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_get_state:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Variant` **endpoint_get_state**\ (\ endpoint\: :godot:`RID`, state\: :ref:`EndpointState<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EndpointState>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_get_state>`

What the last probe or listing returned for the row ``endpoint`` names, named by ``state``. :ref:`ENDPOINT_STATE_STATUS<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_ENDPOINT_STATE_STATUS>` returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.FAILED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_FAILED>` before anything has probed the row, which is not the same as a probe that failed.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_list:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array` **endpoint_list**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_list>`

Every row this session knows how to reach as an :godot:`RID` handle, caller-added rows first and then whatever a listing published. This is what a server browser draws.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_probe:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **endpoint_probe**\ (\ endpoint\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_probe>`

Probes the row ``endpoint`` names without joining it, announcing :ref:`endpoint_updated<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_endpoint_updated>` when it returns. What it found is read back through :ref:`endpoint_get_state()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_get_state>`.

Re-probes every endpoint and emits :ref:`endpoint_updated<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_endpoint_updated>` for each result. Probes run serially.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_set_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **endpoint_set_param**\ (\ endpoint\: :godot:`RID`, param\: :ref:`EndpointParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EndpointParam>`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_endpoint_set_param>`

Writes ``value`` onto the row ``endpoint`` names, announcing :ref:`endpoint_updated<class_NetwMultiplayer_signal_endpoint_updated>`. :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST>` when no such row exists, :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER>` for any ``param`` but :ref:`ENDPOINT_PARAM_DISPLAY_NAME<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_ENDPOINT_PARAM_DISPLAY_NAME>`, which is the only field a caller may rewrite.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                     the row was written
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST     no such row exists
    ┖╴ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER  param names anything but ENDPOINT_PARAM_DISPLAY_NAME

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_add_property_set:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **entity_add_property_set**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, set\: :godot:`RID`, comp\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_add_property_set>`

Attaches the sealed property ``set`` to ``entity``'s ``comp`` address and registers it with the sync pipeline.

Only a sealed set may be attached, because sealing is what fixes membership order, and membership order is wire order. A set that could still gain a column could not be decoded by a peer that attached it earlier.

\ ``set`` is created by :ref:`property_set_create()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_property_set_create>` and sealed by :ref:`property_set_seal()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_property_set_seal>`. ``comp`` is the registration-time component id, ``0`` for the entity root, and one set may be attached at each ``comp``.

Returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST>` when ``entity`` or ``set`` names nothing valid, :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_INVALID_DATA <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_INVALID_DATA>` when the set exists but was never sealed, and :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNAVAILABLE <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNAVAILABLE>` when ``comp`` resolves to no live node on this entity.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the set was attached
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  entity or set names nothing valid
    ┠╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    the set exists but was never sealed
    ┠╴ERR_UNAVAILABLE     comp resolves to no live node on this entity
    ┖╴ERR_UNCONFIGURED    the session has no sync pipeline

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_admit:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`int` **entity_admit**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_admit>`

Reserves the next route and binds it to ``entity``, the server-side counterpart to a client learning a route off the wire. Returns the route on success, and ``0`` both off server authority and for an ``entity`` :ref:`entity_create()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_create>` never created. An entity that already carries a route returns that route again rather than reserving a second one.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_adopt:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_adopt**\ (\ root\: :godot:`Object`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_adopt>`

Takes one node that is already in the scene into replication, returning its entity handle.

The entity is armed where it stands rather than reconstructed, so peers that already hold the node keep it. An invalid :godot:`RID` means the pipeline declined it.

The twin of :ref:`entity_replicate()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_replicate>`, and the two are not interchangeable. This one takes a node the tree already holds and enrols it where it stands, while :ref:`entity_replicate()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_replicate>` rejects such a node and builds one from a scene recipe.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_node:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **entity_bind_node**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, node\: :godot:`Node`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_node>`

Attaches ``node`` as the scene representation of ``entity``, arming it as a :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>` and binding it to the route ``entity`` already holds. Returns :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST>` for a handle :ref:`entity_create()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_create>` never created, :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_INVALID_DATA <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_INVALID_DATA>` for a null ``node`` or for an entity with no route yet, :godot:`@GlobalScope.OK <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_OK>` again for the same node re-bound to the same entity, and :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_ALREADY_EXISTS <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_ALREADY_EXISTS>` when a different node already holds the binding.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the node was bound, or was already this entity's node
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  entity names a handle entity_create never created
    ┠╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    node is null, or entity has no route yet
    ┖╴ERR_ALREADY_EXISTS  a different node already holds the binding

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_route:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **entity_bind_route**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, route\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_route>`

Assigns ``route`` to ``entity``, the route a peer already knows the entity by rather than one this session would reserve on its own. Rejects with :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST>` for an unminted ``entity``, :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_INVALID_DATA <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_INVALID_DATA>` for a ``route`` that is not positive, and :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_ALREADY_IN_USE <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_ALREADY_IN_USE>` when the route is already bound to a different entity.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the route was assigned
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  entity is unminted
    ┠╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    route is not positive
    ┖╴ERR_ALREADY_IN_USE  route is already bound to a different entity

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_call:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **entity_call**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, comp\: :godot:`int`, method\: :godot:`StringName`, args\: :godot:`Array`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_call>`

Calls ``method`` on ``entity``'s ``comp`` component over the wire, addressed the way :ref:`rpc_call()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_rpc_call>` addresses anything. It goes by route to ``peer``, or to every peer that may see the entity when ``peer`` is zero.

Each rejection has a distinct result and occurs before anything is sent. Rejected calls are not counted as dropped packets.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the call was sent
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  no component node and the entity is not live, or the node has no such method at all
    ┠╴ERR_UNAVAILABLE     the entity is live but this session resolves no node for that component yet
    ┖╴ERR_UNCONFIGURED    the node has the method, and nothing declared it remote

\ Either a :ref:`Netw.configure_rpc()<class_Netw_method_configure_rpc>` declaration or the engine's own ``@rpc`` annotation satisfies this. A node with no script can satisfy neither, because both live on a script.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_create:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_create**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_create>`

Creates a fresh entity handle and returns its :godot:`RID`. The handle carries no route, no node and no state beyond :ref:`ENTITY_STATE_UNKNOWN<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_ENTITY_STATE_UNKNOWN>` until :ref:`entity_bind_route()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_route>` or :ref:`entity_admit()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_admit>` gives it one.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_describe:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Dictionary` **entity_describe**\ (\ route\: :godot:`int`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_describe>`

What this session holds about ``route``, as one read a watcher can put beside an event record. ``stage`` carries a :ref:`Stage<enum_NetwEntity_Stage>` value and ``liveness`` carries an :ref:`EntityState<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EntityState>` value.

.. code:: text

    Dictionary
    ┠╴route        int              the route asked about
    ┠╴entity_id    StringName       the durable label
    ┠╴peer_id      int              the peer this entity represents, 0 for a server-owned one
    ┠╴stage        int              the entity's Stage
    ┠╴controller   int              the peer that steers it, 0 for the server
    ┠╴liveness     int              the entity's EntityState
    ┖╴layers       Array            the interest layers it belongs to, in join order

\ Empty for a route this session holds no record for. A lifecycle event names its subject and not its state, because attaching a projection to every event costs every session that emits one. This is the read that returns the state, and :ref:`EVENT_DESPAWNED<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_EVENT_DESPAWNED>` carries the last one instead, since a route outlives its entity by exactly one event.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_despawn:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **entity_despawn**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, opts\: :ref:`NetwDespawnOpts<class_NetwDespawnOpts>` = null\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_despawn>`

Ends ``entity`` on every peer that holds it, returning :godot:`@GlobalScope.OK <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_OK>` when the tear-down was accepted.

This ends the entity rather than hiding it. The route is retired and never reissued, so a receiver drops its row instead of expecting the entity back. To stop one peer seeing an entity that is still alive, use :ref:`interest_leave()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_leave>`, which can be undone. Rejected with :godot:`@GlobalScope.ERR_UNAUTHORIZED <@GlobalScope#class_@GlobalScope_constant_ERR_UNAUTHORIZED>` off server authority, because a client that could end another peer's entity could end any of them.

\ ``opts`` carries the linger window, the save flush and the deferred-free choice for this one call. It is the options of a call rather than an authored policy, which is what :ref:`NetwDespawnConfig<class_NetwDespawnConfig>` is.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the tear-down was accepted
    ┠╴ERR_UNAUTHORIZED    called off server authority
    ┖╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  entity resolves to no view

\ **Server Only.**

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_from_route:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_from_route**\ (\ route\: :godot:`int`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_from_route>`

The entity handle bound to ``route``, or a null :godot:`RID` when no live entity holds that route. The inverse of :ref:`entity_get_route()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_route>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_epoch:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`int` **entity_get_epoch**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_epoch>`

The epoch ``entity``'s route is currently on, which advances each time the same route is reissued to a new identity after a tombstone. A route not yet issued returns its epoch as it stands before any admission.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_node:

The :godot:`Node` bound to ``entity`` through :ref:`entity_bind_node()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_bind_node>`, or ``null`` for a handle with no scene representation.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_parent:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_get_parent**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_parent>`

The entity handle for ``entity``'s parent entity, or a null :godot:`RID` when ``entity`` has no bound node, no parent entity, or a parent whose node has since left the scene.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_peer:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`int` **entity_get_peer**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_peer>`

The peer id ``entity``'s bound node names as its controller, or ``0`` for a handle with no bound node.

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_route:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`int` **entity_get_route**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_route>`

The route ``entity`` is bound to, or ``0`` when it has none yet. This is the address :ref:`entity_call()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_call>` and the sync and spawn channels use to reach the entity over the wire.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_state:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`EntityState<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EntityState>` **entity_get_state**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_state>`

Where ``entity``'s route sits on the :ref:`EntityState<enum_NetwMultiplayer_EntityState>` ladder, as this peer currently knows it. A handle with no route returns :ref:`ENTITY_STATE_UNKNOWN<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_ENTITY_STATE_UNKNOWN>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_view:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>` **entity_get_view**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_get_view>`

The live wrapper carrying ``entity``, or ``null``. A wrapper a death retired is not live and returns ``null`` here, for the rest of the cycle in which it was retired.

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_grant_control:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **entity_grant_control**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_grant_control>`

Hands control of ``entity`` to ``peer``. A no-op off server authority or for a handle with no bound node.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_of:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_of**\ (\ node\: :godot:`Object`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_of>`

The entity handle ``node`` stands for, adopting the record on the way.

It is an adopt over the record at or above ``node``, which is one verb rather than two because every caller wanted both halves, and a caller that took only the first would hold a handle the index does not.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_replicate:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **entity_replicate**\ (\ node\: :godot:`Object`, owner\: :godot:`Object` = null\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_replicate>`

Arms ``node`` for replicated construction and returns its entity handle.

An invalid :godot:`RID` means the pipeline declined the node, which is the same result a session with no pipeline installed gives.

\ ``node`` must not be inside the tree yet. Its identity is taken before it enters, so a receiver rebuilding it from the scene recipe agrees about which entity arrived. :ref:`entity_adopt()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_entity_adopt>` is the call for a node already mounted.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_arm:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **event_arm**\ (\ enabled\: :godot:`bool`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_arm>`

Widens event capture to every value of :ref:`Event<enum_NetwMultiplayer_Event>` when ``enabled`` is ``true``, and narrows it back to only what an installed :ref:`event_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch>` names when ``false``.

While disarmed, an event no watch names is neither recorded nor delivered, so a session nobody is observing pays nothing for this. Arming is what lets :ref:`event_ring()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_ring>` return a result for a route no watch was installed on.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_ring:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array`\[:godot:`Dictionary`\] **event_ring**\ (\ route\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_ring>`

Drains and returns everything the session recorded on ``route``, oldest first, as event records shaped exactly as :ref:`event_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch>` documents. Route ``0`` is the session's own history, since a ring is kept per route.

Reading drains. The ring is emptied by the call, so a second read returns nothing the first already took, and an observer that must not lose a row reads this on a schedule of its own.

A record is created only for a row that is drained, so a ring nobody reads costs nothing but the row. Each drained row is a copy, so writing one reaches neither the ring nor another reader.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_unwatch:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **event_unwatch**\ (\ id\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_unwatch>`

Removes the watch :ref:`event_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch>` returned ``id`` for, returning ``true`` when a watch was there to remove and ``false`` otherwise.

An event that watch alone kept armed stops being recorded once no other watch names it and :ref:`event_arm()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_arm>` does not hold it open.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`int` **event_watch**\ (\ events\: :godot:`PackedInt64Array`, target\: :godot:`Dictionary` = {}, predicate\: :godot:`Dictionary` = {}, sink\: :godot:`Callable` = Callable(), opts\: :godot:`Dictionary` = {}\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch>`

Installs a watch for every value of :ref:`Event<enum_NetwMultiplayer_Event>` in ``events`` and returns its id, which :ref:`event_unwatch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_unwatch>` takes back. ``sink`` is called once per matching act with one record.

::

    var id := api.event_watch(
            [NetwMultiplayer.EVENT_DESPAWNED],
            {route = car_route},
            {},
            _on_despawn)

    func _on_despawn(record: Dictionary) -> void:
        print(record.tick, " ", record.verdict)

\ ``target`` narrows by subject and ``predicate`` by content. ``opts`` carries ``once``, ``dedupe``, ``phase`` and a ``note``.

.. code:: text

    Dictionary
    ┠╴route      int         only acts about this entity
    ┠╴entity_id  StringName  the same, named before a route exists
    ┖╴peer       int         only acts involving this peer

\ Every record carries every key below, so a sink reads by key and never checks for one.

.. code:: text

    Dictionary
    ┠╴event       int         which act, one value of Event
    ┠╴event_name  String      that value's name, empty when it has none
    ┠╴phase       int         0 before the act, 1 after it
    ┠╴tick        int         the session tick the act was raised at
    ┠╴route       int         the subject, 0 when no entity is one
    ┠╴entity_id   StringName  the subject's identity, empty if unknown
    ┠╴peer        int         the peer involved, 0 when none is
    ┠╴verdict     int         the Error returned, OK when not a verdict
    ┠╴detail      Dictionary  what this decision read, keys per event
    ┖╴model       Dictionary  the subject's last state, see below

\ A later version may add a key, so a sink that rejects an unknown one breaks on an addition that costs it nothing.

\ ``detail`` holds what the decision read. A gate carries ``{sender, channel, comp, tick, size}`` and a verdict adds ``{stage, reason}``. It never carries payload bytes and never a :godot:`Node`, whose lifetime would outlive the record.

\ ``model`` is filled only on an event that ends its subject, with the last state recorded while that subject existed, so the worst stop in the system has the whole final state in hand. Every other event leaves it empty, because its subject is alive and one named read away.

\ ``entity_id`` is filled from the last identity seen on the route, so a watch naming an entity by id still matches the one event that ends its life, which knows only the route.

A sink is called for its effect and its return value is discarded. Which watches see one act is decided before the first sink runs, so installing or withdrawing a watch inside a sink changes the next act and never the one it is handling. ``detail`` and ``model`` are each sink's own copy. An act raised off the main thread is delivered after the fact, because a sink runs on the main thread only.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watches:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array`\[:godot:`Dictionary`\] **event_watches**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watches>`

Every watch this session holds, one row each, carrying the watch's id, the events it asked for, its target and predicate, its options and its hit count. :ref:`event_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_event_watch>` is what installs one.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_get_authenticating_peers:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`PackedInt32Array` **get_authenticating_peers**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_get_authenticating_peers>`

The ids of every peer whose connection has completed the transport handshake but not yet finished authentication. Returns an empty array before a :godot:`MultiplayerPeer` is installed.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_admits:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **interest_admits**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_admits>`

Whether ``entity`` is currently visible to ``peer``, read from the committed interest matrix :ref:`interest_flush_now()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_flush_now>` last folded. Returns ``false`` when ``peer`` is not a live registered peer or ``entity`` is not registered, rather than treating either as admitted by default.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_entity_has_filter:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **interest_entity_has_filter**\ (\ entity\: :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_entity_has_filter>`

Whether ``entity`` is subject to interest filtering at all, rather than being open to every peer by default. On the server this reads whether ``entity`` belongs to any committed layer. Off the server it reads whether the local :ref:`NetwEntity.interest<class_NetwEntity_property_interest>` declaration names any label. An entity that returns ``false`` here needs no :ref:`interest_wire_admits()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_wire_admits>` check, because nothing has ever narrowed who receives it.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_explain:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`String` **interest_explain**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_explain>`

A short, stable string naming why ``entity`` is or is not visible to ``peer``, read by walking the same admission chain :ref:`interest_admits()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_admits>` tests. Returns ``"peer is not registered"`` or ``"entity is not registered"`` when either identity does not resolve, one of the intent- or layer-denial reasons naming the route that stopped it, or ``"admitted"`` when nothing denies it.

This is a diagnostic string for logging and tests, not a stable API contract to branch on.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_flush_now:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **interest_flush_now**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_flush_now>`

Folds every staged driver mutation into the committed interest matrix at once, in the caller's frame, rather than waiting for the settle a cascade of admissions, releases and layer mutations coalesces under.

The committed matrix is what :ref:`interest_admits()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_admits>`, :ref:`interest_get_row()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_row>` and every send verdict read, so a layer edge written and not yet flushed is a mutation nothing can observe. This is the verb that makes it observable now.

::

    layer.add_viewer(peer)
    api.interest_flush_now()
    assert(api.interest_admits(entity, peer))

\ A layer driver installed through :ref:`interest_layer_set_driver_callback()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_driver_callback>` can reject the fold.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the matrix committed
    ┠╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    a driver returned something other than an Array
    ┖╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  a driver named an entity this session does not hold

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_membership:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array`\[:godot:`RID`\] **interest_get_membership**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_membership>`

The interest layers ``entity`` currently belongs to, as :godot:`RID` handles opened on demand for any committed layer that has none open yet. Reading this never changes what ``entity`` is admitted to, only which layers already have a live :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` view.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_row:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`PackedInt64Array` **interest_get_row**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_row>`

The committed admission row for ``entity``, a bitset with one bit per live peer sized to the peer table :ref:`interest_flush_now()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_flush_now>` last committed. :ref:`interest_admits()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_admits>` tests one bit of exactly this row. Returns an empty array when ``entity`` is not registered.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_is_filtered:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **interest_is_filtered**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_is_filtered>`

Whether ``entity`` is subject to a visibility filter at all, which is what lets a send skip the per-peer verdict for an entity nothing narrows.

On server authority the result is the committed row, so an entity no layer names is unfiltered however it was declared. Off the server the committed row is empty until the server admits the entity, so the result is the entity's own declared labels, read from the interest facet's declaration.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_join:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_join**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_join>`

Adds ``entity`` to the declaration for ``layer_id``. If ``entity`` is live on server authority, the layer's live membership changes in the same call. The declaration is stored on the entity record, so authoring performed before session attachment survives packing, tree exits, and re-entry.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` **interest_layer**\ (\ name\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer>`

Returns the :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` addressed by ``name``, opening it in the session's interest engine on first use. A second call with the same ``name`` returns a handle to the same layer, so declaring a layer twice never splits its membership.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_entity:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **interest_layer_add_entity**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_entity>`

Puts ``entity`` into ``layer``, so every viewer of that layer becomes a peer this entity is sent to.

The write is staged rather than applied. It lands at the next interest flush, which is what lets a run of membership changes settle together. :ref:`interest_flush_now()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_flush_now>` forces it in the caller's own frame.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the entity was staged into the layer
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  layer or entity names nothing valid
    ┠╴ERR_UNAUTHORIZED    called off server authority
    ┖╴ERR_UNAVAILABLE     the layer rejected the entity

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_viewer:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Error <@GlobalScope#enum_@globalscope_Error>` **interest_layer_add_viewer**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_viewer>`

Makes ``peer`` a viewer of ``layer``, so it is sent every entity the layer holds.

Viewing and membership are the two halves of a layer and neither implies the other. A peer can view a layer it has no entity in, and an entity can sit in a layer nobody views. The write is staged, as it is for :ref:`interest_layer_add_entity()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_entity>`.

.. code:: text

    Error
    ┠╴OK                  the peer was staged as a viewer
    ┠╴ERR_DOES_NOT_EXIST  layer names nothing valid
    ┖╴ERR_INVALID_DATA    peer is 0

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_create:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **interest_layer_create**\ (\ name\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_create>`

Opens the layer called ``name`` and returns its handle, creating it when no layer holds that name yet.

Create-on-demand is the difference between this and :ref:`interest_layer_find()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_find>`, which only looks. Asking twice for one name returns the same handle, so a caller that cannot know whether it is first does not have to check.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_find:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`RID` **interest_layer_find**\ (\ name\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_find>`

The handle of the layer called ``name``, or an invalid :godot:`RID` when no layer holds that name.

This only looks. :ref:`interest_layer_create()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_create>` is the half that opens one, and keeping the two apart is what lets a caller ask whether a layer exists without bringing it into being by asking.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_free:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_layer_free**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_free>`

Drops ``layer`` and everything it held, which is its viewers, its entities, its params and its callbacks.

An entity in no surviving layer stops being sent to anyone who only saw it through this one. Freeing a handle that names no layer is not an error.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_ids:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array`\[:godot:`StringName`\] **interest_layer_ids**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_ids>`

Returns a copy of the layer labels declared for ``entity``. Clearing the returned array does not change the declaration.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_named:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` **interest_layer_named**\ (\ name\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_named>`

Returns the :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` already open under ``name``, or a null reference if nothing has opened it yet. Unlike :ref:`interest_layer()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer>` this never creates one, so it is the read a caller reaches for when opening a layer as a side effect would be wrong.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_remove_viewer:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_layer_remove_viewer**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, peer\: :godot:`int`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_remove_viewer>`

Stops ``peer`` viewing ``layer``. A peer that was not a viewer is not an error.

Whether the peer stops seeing an entity depends on the rest of its membership. An entity it still reaches through another layer keeps arriving. What happens to the entities it no longer sees is the layer's :ref:`LeavePolicy<enum_NetwMultiplayer_LeavePolicy>`, set through :ref:`interest_layer_set_param()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_param>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_driver_callback:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_layer_set_driver_callback**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, callback\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_driver_callback>`

Installs ``callback`` as the driver that decides ``layer``'s membership, replacing whatever hand-written membership the layer held.

A driver runs once per interest flush and returns the whole membership for that pass, which is what makes a rule-based layer (everything within a radius, everything in a room) one function rather than a stream of add and remove calls. Passing an invalid :godot:`Callable` removes the driver and returns the layer to hand-written membership.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_monitor_callback:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_layer_set_monitor_callback**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, callback\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_monitor_callback>`

Installs ``callback`` as ``layer``'s observer, called as entities and viewers enter and leave it.

This watches and does not decide. It cannot change membership, which is what :ref:`interest_layer_set_driver_callback()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_driver_callback>` installs, and a watcher that changed the layer it watches would feed itself. The callback is dropped when :ref:`interest_layer_free()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_free>` frees the layer, so it never outlives what it watches.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_param:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_layer_set_param**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`, param\: :ref:`LayerParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_LayerParam>`, value\: :godot:`Variant`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_set_param>`

Writes one authored setting on ``layer``, selected by ``param`` from :ref:`LayerParam<enum_NetwMultiplayer_LayerParam>`.

These are the layer's rules rather than its contents. They say what happens to the entities a departing viewer stops seeing, and whether those entities keep being drawn. :ref:`interest_layer_add_entity()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_entity>` and :ref:`interest_layer_add_viewer()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_add_viewer>` are the contents.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_view:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` **interest_layer_view**\ (\ layer\: :godot:`RID`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_view>`

A read-only :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` over ``layer``, or ``null`` when the handle names no layer.

It is a view rather than a copy. It reads the live layer, so a caller holding one sees membership as it changes rather than as it stood when the view was taken.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layers:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array` **interest_layers**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layers>`

Returns every :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` this session currently holds open, in no particular order. A layer cleared from the interest engine still appears here until :ref:`interest_layer_free()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_layer_free>` releases its handle.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_leave:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_leave**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_leave>`

Removes ``entity`` from the declaration for ``layer_id``. If ``entity`` is live on server authority, the layer's live membership changes in the same call.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_monitor_snapshot:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Dictionary` **interest_monitor_snapshot**\ (\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_monitor_snapshot>`

How many layers, entities and viewers there are, and how much they are changing, across every :ref:`NetwInterestLayer<class_NetwInterestLayer>` this session holds.

.. code:: text

    Dictionary
    ┠╴layers                 int  declared layer count
    ┠╴entities_filtered      int  entities carrying membership
    ┠╴visible_edges          int  admitted (entity, peer) pairs
    ┠╴dirty_entities         int  entities awaiting the next flush
    ┠╴relay_backlog          int  pending liveness relay work
    ┠╴transitions_total      int  summed enter plus exit since startup
    ┖╴vanished_dirty_skips   int  dirty entities skipped because they left first

\ ``transitions_total`` and ``vanished_dirty_skips`` are cumulative, so a monitor reads them as a delta over an interval to surface churn.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_enter:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_on_enter**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`, callback\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_enter>`

Registers ``callback`` for visibility entries through ``layer_id`` on ``entity``. The callback receives ``(layer_id, peer_id)``.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_leave:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_on_leave**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`, callback\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_leave>`

Registers ``callback`` for visibility exits through ``layer_id`` on ``entity``. The callback receives ``(layer_id, peer_id)``.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_leave_policy:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_on_leave_policy**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`, policy\: :ref:`LeavePolicy<enum_NetwMultiplayer_LeavePolicy>`, custom_callback\: :godot:`Callable` = Callable()\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_leave_policy>`

Sets the :ref:`LeavePolicy<enum_NetwMultiplayer_LeavePolicy>` for ``entity`` on ``layer_id``. :ref:`LEAVE_POLICY_CUSTOM<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_LEAVE_POLICY_CUSTOM>` requires ``custom_callback``, called with ``(peer_id, layer_id)`` on server authority. Other policies reject a callback.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_perception_policy:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **interest_on_perception_policy**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName`, policy\: :ref:`PerceptionPolicy<enum_NetwMultiplayer_PerceptionPolicy>`, custom_callback\: :godot:`Callable` = Callable()\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_on_perception_policy>`

Sets the local :ref:`PerceptionPolicy<enum_NetwMultiplayer_PerceptionPolicy>` for ``entity`` on ``layer_id``. :ref:`PERCEPTION_POLICY_CUSTOM<class_NetwMultiplayer_constant_PERCEPTION_POLICY_CUSTOM>` requires ``custom_callback``, which receives ``(visible, peer_id, layer_id)``. A live entity reapplies perception immediately.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_participant_sees:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **interest_participant_sees**\ (\ peer_id\: :godot:`int`, entity\: :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_participant_sees>`

Whether ``peer_id`` can see ``entity`` as an ordinary participant, holding a listen-server host to the same rule as any client. On the server this reads ``peer_id``'s row through :ref:`interest_get_row()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_get_row>`. Off the server it can only return a result for the local peer, from its own :ref:`NetwEntity.interest<class_NetwEntity_property_interest>` declaration. Game code asking whether a player can see an entity calls here. :ref:`interest_wire_admits()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_wire_admits>` is the one to call when the server must not be blinded to its own entities.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_resolved_layer_ids:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array` **interest_resolved_layer_ids**\ (\ entity\: :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_resolved_layer_ids>`

The :godot:`StringName` ids of every layer ``entity`` currently belongs to, sorted for stable iteration. On the server this reads the committed membership. Off the server, when the entity carries no membership yet, it returns the labels declared on the local :ref:`NetwEntity.interest<class_NetwEntity_property_interest>`.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_shared_entities:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`Array`\[:godot:`Object`\] **interest_shared_entities**\ (\ entity\: :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>`, layer_id\: :godot:`StringName` = &""\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_shared_entities>`

Every other live :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>` that shares a layer with ``entity``, excluding ``entity`` itself and ordered by :ref:`NetwEntity.entity_id<class_NetwEntity_property_entity_id>`. An empty ``layer_id`` searches every layer :ref:`interest_resolved_layer_ids()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_resolved_layer_ids>` returns for ``entity``. A named layer that ``entity`` does not itself belong to returns no entities. On the server this reads the committed membership. Off the server, when that is empty, it scans this peer's own live entities and keeps the ones whose declared labels overlap.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_wire_admits:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **interest_wire_admits**\ (\ peer_id\: :godot:`int`, entity\: :ref:`NetwEntity<class_NetwEntity>`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_wire_admits>`

Whether traffic for ``entity`` may reach ``peer_id``, short-circuiting ``true`` for :godot:`MultiplayerPeer.TARGET_PEER_SERVER <MultiplayerPeer#class_MultiplayerPeer_constant_TARGET_PEER_SERVER>` because server authority always knows every entity it holds. Every other peer falls through to :ref:`interest_participant_sees()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_participant_sees>`. Replication and spawn gating call this rather than :ref:`interest_participant_sees()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_interest_participant_sees>`, because a wire gate that hid the server's own entities from itself would stall authority rather than protect it.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_deny_action:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **lagcomp_deny_action**\ (\ requester\: :godot:`int`, key\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_deny_action>`

Rejects the action ``key`` names and puts the requester back where it was.

A remote requester is told over the wire, so the peer that speculated on the action reverts its own effect. A local one, and one whose peer has already left, is settled here by discarding the effect ``key`` armed. Either way the rejection is the same event, which is why callers never branch on where the requester sits.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_adopt:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **lagcomp_effect_adopt**\ (\ key\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_adopt>`

Confirms the armed act ``key``, dropping its revert and returning every :ref:`lagcomp_effect_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_watch>` on it. An act that is not armed is nothing to confirm.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_arm:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **lagcomp_effect_arm**\ (\ key\: :godot:`StringName`, revert\: :godot:`Callable`, timeout_ticks\: :godot:`int` = 0\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_arm>`

Arms the optimistic act ``key``, parking ``revert`` against a deadline.

The deadline is the session's own tick plus ``timeout_ticks``, so an act armed during a pump expires a whole wait later rather than at whatever tick a caller happened to read first. Zero takes the session default.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_discard:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

|void| **lagcomp_effect_discard**\ (\ key\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_discard>`

Denies the armed act ``key``, running the revert :ref:`lagcomp_effect_arm()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_arm>` parked and returning every :ref:`lagcomp_effect_watch()<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_watch>` on it. An act that is not armed is nothing to deny.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_key:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`StringName` **lagcomp_effect_key**\ (\ entity\: :godot:`RID`, tick\: :godot:`int`, slot\: :godot:`int` = 0\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_key>`

The key naming one predicted act, which is the one ``entity`` armed at ``tick`` in ``slot``.

The three together are the identity, which is what lets a peer that armed an act locally recognise the server's verdict for that same act rather than for a neighbouring one. ``slot`` separates two actions armed by different authorities at a single view tick, and :ref:`Netw.action()<class_Netw_method_action>` is what assigns it.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_pending:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **lagcomp_effect_pending**\ (\ key\: :godot:`StringName`\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_pending>`

Whether the act ``key`` is armed and has neither been adopted nor discarded nor expired.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_watch:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **lagcomp_effect_watch**\ (\ key\: :godot:`StringName`, confirmed\: :godot:`Callable`, denied\: :godot:`Callable`\ ) :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_effect_watch>`

Watches the armed act ``key``, returning ``false`` when it is not armed.

A watcher can therefore never outlive the act it observes.

.. rst-class:: classref-item-separator

----

.. _class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_is_configured:

.. rst-class:: classref-method

:godot:`bool` **lagcomp_is_configured**\ (\ ) |const| :ref:`🔗<class_NetwMultiplayer_method_lagcomp_is_configured>`
